# e.3.1 proof of proposition 2.4

first, we recall the definition of the general function class ucb algorithm. let  $z_{t} = (x_{t},a_{t})$  and  $\mathcal{z}_t = \{z_1,\ldots ,z_t\}$ . define  $\| f\|_{\mathcal{z}}^2 = \sum_{z\in \mathcal{z}}f^2 (z)$ . then the algorithm is defined as follows. at round  $t$ :

- set  $\widehat{f}_t = \arg \min_{f\in \mathcal{f}}\sum_{i < t}\bigl (f(x_i,a_i) - r_i(a_i)\bigr)^2$  
- define  $\mathcal{f}_t = \left\{f\in \mathcal{f}:\| f - \widehat{f_t}\|_{\mathcal{z}_{t - 1}}\leq \beta_t\right\}$ .  
- choose  $a_{t} = \arg \max_{a\in \mathcal{a}}\max_{f\in \mathcal{f}_{t}}f(x_{t},a)$

from russo and van roy (2013), proposition 2, we are guaranteed that if  $\beta_{t} = \beta \coloneqq \sqrt{c_{1}\cdot\log(|\mathcal{f}| / \delta)}$  for all  $t$  for some absolute constant  $c_1$ , then  $f^{\star}\in \mathcal{f}_t$  for all  $t$  with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ . let  $[a]_{\delta} = a\mathbb{i}\{a > \delta \}$ . conditioned on this event, and using that  $f^{\star}$  has gap  $\delta$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t} f ^ {\star} \left(x _ {t}, \pi^ {\star} \left(x _ {t}\right)\right) - f ^ {\star} \left(x _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) = \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t} \left[ f ^ {\star} \left(x _ {t}, \pi^ {\star} \left(x _ {t}\right)\right) - f ^ {\star} \left(x _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \right] _ {\delta / 2} \\ \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t} \sup  _ {f \in \mathcal {f} _ {t}} [ f (x _ {t}, \pi^ {\star} (x _ {t})) - f ^ {\star} (x _ {t}, a _ {t}) ] _ {\delta / 2} \\ \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t} \sup  _ {f \in \mathcal {f} _ {t}} [ f (x _ {t}, a _ {t}) - f ^ {\star} (x _ {t}, a _ {t}) ] _ {\delta / 2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

in particular, let us define  $\overline{\mathcal{f}}_t = \left\{f\in \mathcal{f}:\| f - f^\star \|_{\mathcal{z}_{t - 1}}\leq 2\beta_t\right\}$ . then by triangle inequality,  $\mathcal{f}_t\subseteq \overline{\mathcal{f}}_t$ , so if we define  $w_{t}(z) = \sup_{f\in \overline{\mathcal{f}}_{t}}[f(z) - f^{\star}(z)]$ , then

$$
\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t} f ^ {\star} \left(x _ {t}, \pi^ {\star} \left(x _ {t}\right)\right) - f ^ {\star} \left(x _ {t}, a _ {t}\right) \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t} w _ {t} \left(z _ {t}\right) \mathbb {i} \left\{w _ {t} \left(z _ {t}\right) > \delta / 2 \right\}.
$$

we now appeal to the following lemma.

lemma e.4 (variant of russo and van roy (2013), lemma 3). for any  $\zeta > 0$ ,

$$
\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t} \mathbb {i} \{w _ {t} (z _ {t}) > \zeta \} \leq \left(\frac {4 \beta^ {2}}{\zeta^ {2}} + 1\right) \check {\mathfrak {e}} _ {f ^ {\star}} ^ {\mathrm {v a l}} (\mathcal {f}, \zeta).
$$

to apply this result, let us order the indices such that  $w_{i_1}(z_{i_1}) \geq w_{i_2}(z_{i_2}) \geq \ldots \geq w_{i_t}(z_{i_t})$ . consider any index  $t$  for which  $w_{i_t}(z_{i_t}) > \delta / 2$ . for any particular  $\zeta > \delta / 2$ , if we have  $w_{i_t}(z_{i_t}) > \zeta$ , then lemma e.4 (since  $\zeta \leq 1 \leq \beta$ ) implies that

$$
t \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t} \mathbb {i} \left\{w _ {t} \left(z _ {t}\right) > \zeta \right\} \leq \frac {5 \beta^ {2}}{\zeta^ {2}} \check {\mathfrak {e}} _ {f ^ {\star}} ^ {\text {v a l}} (\mathcal {f}, \zeta). \tag {90}
$$

since we have restricted to  $\zeta \geq \delta /2$ , rearranging yields

$$
w _ {i _ {t}} (z _ {i _ {t}}) \leq \sqrt {\frac {5 \beta^ {2} \mathfrak {e} _ {f ^ {*}} ^ {\mathrm {v a l}} (\mathcal {f} , \delta / 2)}{t}}.
$$

now, let  $t_0$  be the greatest index  $t$  such that  $w_{i_t}(z_{i_t}) > \delta / 2$ . then we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t} w _ {t} \left(z _ {t}\right) \mathbb {i} \left\{w _ {t} \left(z _ {t}\right) > \delta / 2 \right\} = \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t _ {0}} w _ {t} \left(z _ {t}\right) \mathbb {i} \left\{w _ {t} \left(z _ {t}\right) > \delta / 2 \right\} \\ \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t _ {0}} \sqrt {\frac {5 \beta^ {2} \mathfrak {e} _ {f ^ {\star}} ^ {\mathrm {v a l}} (\mathcal {f} , \delta / 2)}{t}} \\ \leq \sqrt {5 \beta^ {2} \mathfrak {e} _ {f ^ {\star}} ^ {\mathrm {v a l}} (\mathcal {f} , \delta / 2) t _ {0}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

we know that from (90) that  $t_0 \leq \frac{20\beta^2}{\delta^2} \check{\mathfrak{e}}_{f^\star}^{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal{f}, \delta / 2)$ , so altogether we have

$$
\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {t} w _ {t} \left(z _ {t}\right) \mathbb {i} \left\{w _ {t} \left(z _ {t}\right) > \delta / 2 \right\} \leq 1 0 0 \frac {\beta^ {2}}{\delta} \mathfrak {e} _ {f ^ {\star}} ^ {\text {v a l}} (\mathcal {f}, \delta / 2).
$$

to conclude, we set  $\delta = 1 / t$ , and the final result follows from the law of total expectation.

proof of lemma e.4. let us adopt the shorthand  $d = \check{\mathfrak{e}}_{f^{\star}}^{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal{f},\zeta)$ . we begin with a definition. we say  $z$  is  $\zeta$ -independent of  $z_1,\ldots ,z_t$  if there exists  $f\in \mathcal{f}$  such that  $|f(z) - f^{\star}(z)| > \zeta$  and  $\sum_{i = 1}^{t}(f(z_i) - f^{\star}(z_i))^2\leq \zeta^2$ . we say  $z$  is  $\zeta$ -dependent on  $z_1,\ldots ,z_t$  if for all  $f\in \mathcal{f}$  with  $\sum_{i = 1}^{t}(f(z_{i}) - f^{\star}(z_{i}))^{2}\leq \zeta^{2}$ ,  $|f(z) - f^{\star}(z)|\leq \zeta$ .

we first claim that for any  $t$ , if  $w_{t}(z_{t}) > \zeta$ , then  $z_{t}$  is  $\zeta$ -dependent on at most  $4\beta^{2} / \zeta^{2}$  disjoint subsequences of  $z_{1}, \ldots, z_{t-1}$ . indeed, let  $f$  be such that  $|f(z_{t}) - f^{\star}(z_{t})| > \zeta$ . if  $z_{t}$  is  $\zeta$ -dependent on a particular subsequence  $z_{i_{1}}, \ldots, z_{i_{k}}$  but  $w_{t}(z_{t}) > \zeta$ , we must have

$$
\sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k} (f (z _ {i _ {j}}) - f ^ {\star} (z _ {i _ {j}})) ^ {2} \geq \zeta^ {2}.
$$

if there are  $m$  such disjoint sequences, we have

$$
m \zeta^ {2} \leq \| f - f ^ {\star} \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {t - 1}} ^ {2} \leq 4 \beta^ {2},
$$

so  $m\leq \frac{4\beta^2}{\zeta^2}$

next we claim that for  $\tau$  and any sequence  $(z_{1},\ldots ,z_{\tau})$ , there is some  $j$  such that  $z_{j}$  is  $\zeta$  dependent on at least  $\lfloor \tau /d\rfloor$  disjoint subsequences of  $z_{1},\ldots ,z_{j - 1}$ . let  $n = \lfloor \tau /d\rfloor$ , and let  $b_{1},\ldots ,b_{n}$  be subsequences of  $z_{1},\ldots ,z_{\tau}$ . we initialize with  $b_{i} = (z_{i})$ . if  $z_{n + 1}$  is  $\zeta$ -dependent on  $b_{i} = (z_{i})$  for all  $1\leq i\leq n$  we are done. otherwise, choose  $i$  such that  $z_{n + 1}$  is  $\zeta$ -independent of  $b_{i}$ , and add it to  $b_{i}$ . repeat this process until we reach  $j$  such that either  $z_{j}$  is  $\zeta$ -dependent on all  $b_{i}$  or  $j = \tau$ . in the first case we are done, while in the second case, we have  $\sum_{i = 1}^{n}|b_i|\geq \tau \geq dn$ . moreover,  $|b_i|\leq d$ , since each  $z_{j}\in b_{i}$  is  $\zeta$ -independent of its prefix. we conclude that  $|b_i| = d$  for all  $i$ , so in this case  $z_{\tau}$  is  $\zeta$ -dependent on all  $b_{i}$ .

finally, let  $(z_{t_1},\ldots ,z_{t_\tau})$  be the subsequence  $z_{1},\dots,z_{t}$  consisting of all elements for which  $w_{i_i}(z_{t_i}) > \zeta$ . each element of the sequence is dependent on at most  $4\beta^{2} / \zeta^{2}$  disjoint subsequences of  $(z_{t_1},\dots,z_{t_\tau})$ , and by the argument above, one element is dependent on at least  $\lfloor \tau /d\rfloor$  disjoint subsequences, so we must have  $\lfloor \tau /d\rfloor \leq 4\beta^2 /\zeta^2$ , and in particular  $\tau \leq (4\beta^{2} / \zeta^{2} + 1)d$ .

# e.3.2 proof of theorem 2.10

this proof closely follows that of theorem 2.7. as with that theorem, we prove a slightly more general result. let  $\mathcal{g} \subseteq (\mathcal{z} \to [0,1])$  be a function class, and let  $\pmb{\theta}_{\mathcal{p}}^{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal{g},\delta,\varepsilon)$  be defined as in (85). let  $\check{\mathfrak{e}}^{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal{g},\delta)$  be the length of the longest sequence of points  $z^{(1)},\ldots,z^{(m)}$  such that for all  $i$ , there exists  $g^{(i)} \in \mathcal{g}$  such that

$$
g ^ {(i)} \left(z ^ {(i)}\right) > \delta , \quad \text {a n d} \quad \sum_ {j <   i} \left(g ^ {(i)}\right) ^ {2} \left(z ^ {(j)}\right) \leq \delta^ {2}.
$$

the value function eluder dimension for  $\mathcal{g}$  is defined as  $\mathfrak{e}^{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal{g},\delta_0) = \sup_{\delta >\delta_0}\check{\mathfrak{e}}^{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal{g},\delta)$ .

we will prove the following result.

theorem e.2. for any uniform glivenko-cantelli class  $\mathcal{g} \subseteq (\mathcal{z} \to [0,1])$

$$
\sup  _ {\mathcal {p}} \sup  _ {\varepsilon > 0} \theta_ {\mathcal {p}} ^ {\text {v a l}} (\mathcal {g}, \delta , \varepsilon) \leq 4 \mathrm {e} ^ {\text {v a l}} (\mathcal {g}, \delta), \quad \forall \delta > 0. \tag {91}
$$

let  $\delta, \varepsilon > 0$  be given. let  $\gamma \leq 1/4$  be fixed. then by lemma e.2. there exists a distribution  $\widehat{\mathcal{p}} = \mathrm{unif}(z_1, \ldots, z_n)$  such that

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\mathcal {p}} \big (\exists g \in \mathcal {g}: g (z) > \delta , \| g \| _ {\mathcal {p}} ^ {2} \leq \varepsilon^ {2} \big) \leq \mathbb {p} _ {\hat {\mathcal {p}}} \big (\exists g \in \mathcal {g}: g (z) > \delta , \| g \| _ {\hat {\mathcal {p}}} ^ {2} \leq \varepsilon^ {2} + \gamma \big) + \gamma .
$$

write  $s = (z_{1},\ldots ,z_{n})$ , so that  $\widehat{\mathcal{p}} = \mathrm{unif}(s)$ . define  $\mathcal{g}_s(\varepsilon) := \left\{g\in \mathcal{g}:\sum_{j = 1}^n g^2 (z_j)\leq \varepsilon^2\right\}$ . then we have

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\widehat {\mathcal {p}}} \big (\exists g \in \mathcal {g}: g (z) > \delta , \| g \| _ {\mathcal {p}} ^ {2} \leq \varepsilon^ {2} + \gamma \big) = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbb {i} \big \{w _ {\mathcal {g} _ {s (\varepsilon^ {\prime})}} (z _ {i}) > \delta \big \},
$$

where  $\varepsilon^{\prime 2} \coloneqq n(\varepsilon^{2} + \gamma)$ . by lemma e.4, we can bound

$$
\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbb {i} \left\{w _ {\mathcal {g} _ {s} \left(\varepsilon^ {\prime}\right)} \left(z _ {i}\right) > \delta \right\} \leq \frac {1}{n} \left(\frac {4 \left(\varepsilon^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}}{\delta^ {2}} + 1\right) \check {\mathfrak {e}} ^ {\text {v a l}} (\mathcal {g}, \delta).
$$

to conclude the result, we take  $\gamma \rightarrow 0$  (and consequently  $n\to \infty$ ), so that the bound above yields

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\mathcal {p}} \left(\exists g \in \mathcal {g}: g (z) > \delta , \| g \| _ {\mathcal {p}} ^ {2} \leq \varepsilon^ {2}\right) \leq \frac {4 \varepsilon^ {2}}{\delta^ {2}} \cdot \check {\mathfrak {e}} ^ {\text {v a l}} (\mathcal {g}, \delta),
$$

as desired.

![](images/7f47a634bb9620f1502f4faf5a597d46eb44f8d5c2aaad10b43e469c6a8a961d.jpg)

# part ii

# proofs for reinforcement learning results

# f proof of theorem 3.1

we let  $\tau^{(k,h)}$  denote the  $h$ th trajectory gathered by the algorithm during iteration  $k$  (i.e., the trajectory obtained by rolling in to layer  $h$  with  $\pi^{(k)}$ , then switching to uniform exploration. throughout the proof, we let  $s_1^{(k,h)},\ldots ,s_h^{(k,h)}$  denote the latent states encountered during  $\tau^{(k,h)}$ , which emphasize are not observed.

we define  $\mathcal{l}_h^{(k)} = \left\{s_h^{(1,h)},\ldots ,s_h^{(k - 1,h)}\right\}$ . for any collection  $\mathcal{l} \subseteq s$ , we define

$$
\| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {l}} ^ {2} = \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {l}} \mathbb {e} _ {x \sim \psi (s), a \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \big (f (x, a) - f ^ {\prime} (x, a) \big) ^ {2}.
$$

we also let  $\mathcal{h}^{(k)} = \left\{(s_1^{(k,h)},x_1^{(k,h)},a_1^{(k,h)},r_1^{(k,h)}),\ldots ,(s_h^{(k,h)},x_h^{(k,h)},a_h^{(k,h)},r_h^{(k,h)})\right\}_{h = 1}^h$  denote the entire history for iteration  $k$ .

let us define an intermediate quantity which is closely related to the value function disagreement coefficient, which we will work with throughout the proof. for each  $s \in s_h$ , define

$$
\begin{array}{l} \breve {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {s} (\mathcal {f} _ {h}, \varepsilon_ {0}) \\ = 1 \vee \sup _ {f ^ {\star} \in \mathcal {f} _ {h}} \sup _ {\varepsilon \geq \varepsilon_ {0}} \frac {\mathbb {e} _ {x \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \sup \big \{| f (x , a) - f ^ {\star} (x , a) | ^ {2} : f \in \mathrm {s t a r} (\mathcal {f} _ {h} , f ^ {\star}) , \| f - f ^ {\star} \| _ {s} \leq \varepsilon \big \}}{\varepsilon^ {2}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

we define  $\check{\theta}_h(\mathcal{f}_h\varepsilon) = \sum_{s\in \mathcal{s}_h}\check{\theta}_s(\delta ,\varepsilon)$  analogously. lastly, we abbreviate  $\check{\theta}_s(\varepsilon)\equiv \check{\theta}_s(\mathcal{f}_h,\varepsilon)$  and  $\check{\theta}_h(\varepsilon)\equiv$ $\check{\theta}_h(\mathcal{f}_h,\varepsilon)$ . we will pass from this quantity to  $\pmb{\theta}^{\mathrm{val}}$  at the end of the proof.

# f.1 confidence sets

let

$$
\widehat {\mathcal {f}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} := \left\{f \in \operatorname {s t a r} \left(\mathcal {f} _ {h}, \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)}\right), \left\| f - \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} \leq \beta_ {h} \right\}
$$

be the set used to compute the upper confidence function  $\overline{\mathbf{q}}_h^{(k)}$  in iteration  $k$ . let the bayes predictor for this round be defined as

$$
\bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x, a) = f ^ {\star} (x, a) + \left[ p _ {h} ^ {\star} \overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right] (x, a).
$$

recall that the optimistic completeness assumption implies that  $\tilde{f}_h^{(k)}\in \mathcal{f}_h$

theorem f.1. for any  $\delta \in (0,1)$ , if we choose  $\beta_{1},\ldots ,\beta_{h}$  in algorithm 2 such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \beta_ {h} ^ {2} = 4 0 0 h ^ {2} \log \left(f _ {\max } k h \delta^ {- 1}\right) \\ \beta_ {h} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{2} \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + 1 5 0 ^ {2} h ^ {2} a ^ {2} \check {\theta} _ {h + 1} (\beta_ {h + 1} k ^ {- 1 / 2}) ^ {2} \log (2 f _ {\max} k h \delta^ {- 1}) + 7 0 0 h ^ {2} s \log (2 e k), \\ \end{array}
$$

then with probability at least  $1 - 3\delta$ ,  $\bar{f}_h^{(k)} \in \widehat{\mathcal{f}}_h^{(k)}$  for all  $k, h$ .

let  $\mathcal{e}_{\mathrm{conf}}$  denote the event from theorem f.1. this event has the following consequence.

lemma f.1. whenever  $\mathcal{e}_{\mathrm{conf}}$  holds, we have

$$
\overline {{\mathbf {q}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x, a) \geq \mathbf {q} _ {h} ^ {\star} (x, a) \tag {92}
$$

for all  $h,k$

proof of lemma f.1. let  $k$  be fixed. we prove the result by induction on  $h$ . first, the property holds trivially for round  $h + 1$ , since  $\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{h + 1}^{(\kappa)} = \mathbf{v}_{h + 1}^{\star} = 0$ .

now, consider a fixed timestep  $h$ , and suppose inductively that the property holds for round  $h + 1$ . then we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \overline {{\mathbf {q}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x, a) = \sup  _ {f \in \widehat {\mathcal {f}} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} f (x, a) \geq \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x, a) = f ^ {\star} (x, a) + \left[ p ^ {\star} \overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right] (x, a) \\ \geq f ^ {\star} (x, a) + \left[ p ^ {\star} \mathbf {v} _ {h + 1} ^ {\star} \right] (x, a) = \mathbf {q} _ {h} ^ {\star} (x, a), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we have used that  $\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{h + 1}^{(k)}(x)\geq \mathbf{v}_{h + 1}^{\star}(x)$  whenever  $\overline{\mathbf{q}}_{h + 1}^{(k)}(x,a)\geq \mathbf{q}_{h + 1}^{\star}(x,a)$  for all  $x,a$ .

# f.2 bounding regret

we now use the concentration guarantees established above to bound the regret of the policies  $\pi^{(1)},\ldots ,\pi^{(k)}$  that is, we wish to bound

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \mathbf {v} ^ {\star} - \mathbf {v} ^ {\pi^ {(k)}}
$$

note that since our algorithm executes policies besides these ones, this is not a bound on the true regret of the algorithm, but rather for an intermediate quantity which is only used for the analysis.

we first state a regret decomposition for  $q$ -functions and induced policies that are optimistic in the following sense.

definition f.1. a  $q$ -function  $\overline{\mathbf{q}}$  and policy  $\pi$  are said to be optimistic if for all  $h \in [h]$ ,

$$
\overline {{\mathbf {q}}} _ {h} (x, a) \geq \mathbf {q} _ {h} ^ {\star} (x, a) \forall x, a \quad a n d \quad \pi (x) \in \operatorname * {a r g   m a x} _ {a \in \mathcal {a}} \overline {{\mathbf {q}}} _ {h} (x, a) \forall x \in \mathcal {x} _ {h}.
$$

we define  $\overline{\mathbf{v}}_h(x) = \max_a\overline{\mathbf{q}}_h(x,a)$  as the induced value function.

define  $\mathsf{clip}[x\mid \varepsilon ] = x\mathbb{i}\{x\geq \varepsilon \}$ . the following lemma bounds the regret of any optimistic pair  $(\overline{\mathbf{q}},\pi)$  in terms of clipped surpluses for  $\overline{\mathbf{q}}$ .

lemma f.2 (simchowitz and jamieson (2019), theorem b.3). let  $(\overline{\mathbf{q}},\pi)$  be optimistic, and let  $\overline{\mathbf{e}}_h(x,a) = \overline{\mathbf{q}}_h(x,a) - \big(f^{\star}(x,a) + [p_h^{\star}\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{h + 1}](x,a)\big)$  be the optimistic surplus. then

$$
\mathbf {v} ^ {\star} - \mathbf {v} ^ {\pi} \leq 2 e \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \mathbb {e} _ {\pi} \left[ \operatorname {c l i p} \left[ \overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right) \mid \check {\delta} \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right) \right] \right], \tag {93}
$$

where  $\bar{\delta} (x,a)\coloneqq \delta (x,a) / 4h$

define

$$
\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x, a) = \overline {{\mathbf {q}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x, a) - \left(f ^ {\star} (x, a) + \left[ p _ {h} ^ {\star} \overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right] (x, a)\right).
$$

then lemma f.1 and lemma f.2 imply that conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}_{\mathrm{conf}}$ , we have

$$
\mathbf {v} ^ {\star} - \mathbf {v} ^ {\pi^ {(k)}} \leq 2 e \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \mathbb {e} _ {\pi^ {(k)}} \left[ \operatorname {c l i p} \left[ \overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right) \mid \check {\delta} \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right) \right] \right]
$$

for all  $k$ .

since  $\operatorname{clip}[x|\varepsilon] \leq \frac{x^2}{\varepsilon}$ , we can further upper bound as

$$
\mathbf {v} ^ {\star} - \mathbf {v} ^ {\pi^ {(k)}} \leq 2 e \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \mathbb {e} _ {\pi^ {(k)}} \left[ \frac {\left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h} , a _ {h}\right)\right) ^ {2}}{\check {\delta} \left(x _ {h} , a _ {h}\right)} \right] = 8 e h \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \mathbb {e} _ {\pi^ {(k)}} \left[ \frac {\left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h} , a _ {h}\right)\right) ^ {2}}{\delta \left(x _ {h} , a _ {h}\right)} \right].
$$

let  $p_h^{(k)}(s) = \mathbb{p}_{\pi^{(k)}}(s_h = s)$ . then we can write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\pi^ {(k)}} \left[ \frac {\left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h} , a _ {h}\right)\right) ^ {2}}{\delta \left(x _ {h} , a _ {h}\right)} \right] = \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h}} p _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \left[ \frac {\left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h} , \pi^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h}\right)\right)\right) ^ {2}}{\delta \left(x _ {h} , \pi^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h}\right)\right)} \right] \\ \leq a \sum_ {s \in s _ {h}} p _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \frac {\left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h} , a _ {h})\right) ^ {2}}{\delta (x _ {h} , a _ {h})} \right] \\ \leq a \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h}} \frac {p _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s)}{\delta (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

altogether, we have

$$
\mathbf {v} ^ {\star} - \mathbf {v} ^ {\pi^ {(k)}} \leq 8 e h a \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h}} \frac {1}{\delta (s)} p _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right)\right) ^ {2} \right]. \tag {94}
$$

# f.3 bounding the surplus

we now focus on bounding the surplus terms

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) ^ {2} \right].
$$

let  $k$ ,  $h$ , and  $s \in s_h$  be fixed. then we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \sup  \bigl \{\bigl (f (x _ {h}, a _ {h}) - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h}) \bigr) ^ {2} \mid f \in \widehat {\mathcal {f}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \bigr \} \\ \leq 2 \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\text {u n i f}}} \sup  \left\{\left(f \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right) - \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right)\right) ^ {2} \mid f \in \widehat {\mathcal {f}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\} (95) \\ + 2 \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(\widehat {f} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right) - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right)\right) ^ {2} \right]. (96) \\ \end{array}
$$

now, for each  $k$ , define

$$
\| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {k, h} ^ {2} = \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \pi^ {(j)}} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \big (f (x _ {h}, a _ {h}) - f ^ {\prime} (x _ {h}, a _ {h}) \big) ^ {2}.
$$

we appeal to the following uniform concentration guarantee.

lemma f.3. with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ , for all  $k, h$ , we have

$$
\| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {k, h} ^ {2} \leq 2 \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} + 8 h ^ {2} \log (2 k h | \mathcal {f} _ {h} | \delta^ {- 1}), \quad \text {f o r a l l} f ^ {\prime} \in \mathcal {f} _ {h}, f \in \operatorname {s t a r} (\mathcal {f} _ {h}, f ^ {\prime}).
$$

let  $\mathcal{e}_{\mathrm{conc}}$  denote the event in lemma f.3. since  $\beta_h^2 \geq 8h^2 \log(2kh|f_h|\delta^{-1})$  for all  $h$ , we have

$$
\sup  _ {f \in \widehat {\mathcal {f}} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} \left\| f - \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {k, h} ^ {2} \leq 3 \beta_ {h} ^ {2}
$$

conditioned on  $\mathcal{e}_{\mathrm{conc}}$

now, we can write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| f - \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {k, h} ^ {2} = \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \pi^ {(j)}} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left(f (x _ {h}, a _ {h}) - \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) ^ {2} \\ = \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h}} \left(\sum_ {j <   k} p _ {h} ^ {(j)} (s)\right) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left(f (x _ {h}, a _ {h}) - \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

in particular, if we define  $q_h^{(k)}(s) = \sum_{j < k} p_h^{(j)}(s)$ , we are guaranteed that for all  $f \in \widehat{\mathcal{f}}_h^{(k)}$ ,

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left(f (x _ {h}, a _ {h}) - \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) ^ {2} \leq \frac {3 \beta_ {h} ^ {2}}{q _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s)} \quad \forall s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h}.
$$

since  $\bar{f}_h^{(k)}\in \widehat{\mathcal{f}}_h^{(k)}$ , this immediately allows us to bound (96) by  $\frac{6\beta_h^2}{q_h^{(k)}(s)}$ . to handle (95) we use the definition of the disagreement coefficient, which gives us that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \sup  \left\{\left(f (x _ {h}, a _ {h}) - \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) ^ {2} \mid f \in \widehat {\mathcal {f}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\} \leq \check {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {s} \left(3 \beta_ {h} ^ {2} / \left(q _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s)\right) ^ {1 / 2}\right) \cdot \frac {3 \beta_ {h} ^ {2}}{q _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s)}. \\ \leq \check {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {s} \left(\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \cdot \frac {3 \beta_ {h} ^ {2}}{q _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we have used that  $\check{\pmb{\theta}}_s(\cdot)$  is non-increasing and  $q_h^{(k)}\leq k$ . since  $\theta \geq 1$ , we conclude that for all  $s$

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right)\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq 1 2 \check {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {s} \left(\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \cdot \frac {\beta_ {h} ^ {2}}{q _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s)}. \tag {97}
$$

# f.4 final regret bound

condition on  $\mathcal{e}_{\mathrm{conf}}$  and  $\mathcal{e}_{\mathrm{conc}}$ , which occur together with probability at least  $1 - 4\delta$ . to bound the total regret across all episodes, we first apply (94) to give

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \mathbf {v} ^ {\star} - \mathbf {v} ^ {\pi^ {(k)}} \leq 8 e h a \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h}} \frac {1}{\delta (s)} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} p _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) ^ {2} \right].
$$

now, consider a fixed state  $s \in s_h$  and let  $k_s = \min \{k : q_h^{(k)}(s) \geq 1\}$ . then we have  $q_h^{(k)}(s) \leq 2$ , so we can bound

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} p _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq \sum_ {k = k _ {s}} ^ {k} p _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) _ {} ^ {2} \right] + 2 h ^ {2}.
$$

we apply (97) to each term in the sum to bound by

$$
\sum_ {k = k _ {s}} ^ {k} p _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq 1 2 \beta_ {h} ^ {2} \check {\pmb {\theta}} _ {s} (\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2}) \sum_ {k = k _ {s}} ^ {k} \frac {p _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s)}{q _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s)}
$$

observe that  $\sum_{k = k_s}^k\frac{p_h^{(k)}(s)}{q_h^{(k)}(s)} = \sum_{k = k_s}^k\frac{q_h^{(k + 1)}(s) - q_h^{(k)}(s)}{q_h^{(k)}(s)}$  and  $q_{h}^{(k_{s})}(s)\geq 1$ . we appeal to the following lemma.

lemma f.4. for any sequence  $1 \leq x_{1}, \leq \ldots, \leq x_{n+1}$  with  $|x_{i} - x_{i+1}| \leq 1$ ,  $\sum_{i=1}^{n} \frac{x_{i+1} - x_{i}}{x_{i}} \leq 2 \log (x_{n+1} / x_{1})$ .

lemma f.4 grants that  $\sum_{k = k_s}^{k}\frac{p_h^{(k)}(s)}{q_h^{(k)}(s)}\leq 2\log (k)$ . altogether, since  $\beta_h\geq h^2$  and  $\theta \geq 1$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} p _ {h} ^ {(k)} (s) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(\overline {{\mathbf {e}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq 1 4 \beta_ {h} ^ {2} \breve {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {s} (\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2}) \log (k).
$$

summing across all states, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \mathbf {v} ^ {\star} - \mathbf {v} ^ {\pi^ {(k)}} \leq 3 1 0 h a \log k \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \beta_ {h} ^ {2} \sum_ {s \in s _ {h}} \frac {\check {\theta} _ {s} \left(\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2}\right)}{\delta (s)} \\ \leq 310 h a \log k \max _ {h} \beta_ {h} ^ {2} \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s}} \frac {\check {\theta} _ {s} \left(\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2}\right)}{\delta (s)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

to simplify further, we use that for all  $h$

$$
\beta_ {h} ^ {2} = \mathcal {o} \left(h ^ {2} a ^ {2} \max  _ {h ^ {\prime}} \check {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {h ^ {\prime}} \left(\beta_ {h ^ {\prime}} k ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) ^ {2} \log \left(f _ {\max } h k \delta^ {- 1}\right) + h ^ {2} s \log (k)\right).
$$

this gives

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \mathbf {v} ^ {\star} - \mathbf {v} ^ {\pi^ {(k)}} \leq \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(h ^ {3} a ^ {3} \max  _ {h} \check {\theta} _ {h} \left(\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) ^ {2} \log \left(f _ {\max }\right) + h ^ {3} a s\right) \cdot \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h}} \frac {\check {\theta} _ {s} \left(\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2}\right)}{\delta (s)} \\ = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \bigg (h ^ {2} a ^ {3} \max _ {h} \check {\pmb {\theta}} _ {h} (\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2}) ^ {2} \log | \mathcal {f} | + h ^ {3} a s \bigg) \cdot \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h}} \frac {\check {\pmb {\theta}} _ {s} (\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2})}{\delta (s)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where have we used that  $\log |\mathcal{f}| = h\log (f_{\mathrm{max}})$ . to deduce the in-expectation error bound, we set  $\delta = 1 / kh$ , so that

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {k} \mathbf {v} ^ {\star} - \mathbf {v} ^ {\pi^ {(k)}} \right] = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \left(h ^ {2} a ^ {3} \max _ {h} \check {\pmb {\theta}} _ {h} (\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2}) ^ {2} \log | \mathcal {f} | + h ^ {3} a s\right) \cdot \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h}} \frac {\check {\pmb {\theta}} _ {s} (\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2})}{\delta (s)},
$$

where we have used that the regret in each episode is bounded by  $h$ . finally, we observe that by dividing both sides above by  $k$ , this is equivalent to

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {v} ^ {\star} - \mathbf {v} ^ {\pi} ] = \widetilde {\mathcal {o}} \bigg (\frac {h ^ {2} a ^ {3} \max _ {h} \check {\pmb {\theta}} _ {h} (\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2}) ^ {2} \log | \mathcal {f} | + h ^ {3} a s}{k} \bigg) \cdot \sum_ {h = 1} ^ {h} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h}} \frac {\check {\pmb {\theta}} _ {s} (\beta_ {h} k ^ {- 1 / 2})}{\delta (s)}.
$$

we now move to the disagreement coefficient defined in (40).

lemma f.5. let  $\mathcal{g}:\mathcal{z}\to [0,r]$  be any function class and  $\mathcal{p}\in \delta (\mathcal{z})$ . let  $\| g\|_{\mathcal{p}}^2 = \mathbb{e}_{\mathcal{p}}[g^2 ]$ , and define

$$
\boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\mathcal {d}; g ^ {\star}} (\mathcal {g}, \varepsilon) = 1 \vee \sup  _ {\varepsilon \geq \varepsilon_ {0}} \frac {\mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} \sup  \left\{\left(g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)\right) ^ {2} : g \in \mathcal {g} , \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} [ \left(g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)\right) ^ {2} ] \leq \varepsilon^ {2} \right\}}{\varepsilon^ {2}}.
$$

then for all  $\varepsilon \in (0,r]$ , we have  $\pmb{\theta}_{\mathcal{d};g^{\star}}(\mathrm{star}(\mathcal{g},g^{\star}),\varepsilon)\leq \pmb{\theta}_{\mathcal{d};g^{\star}}(\mathcal{g},\varepsilon)(16\log (r / \varepsilon) + 8)$ .

lemma f.5 implies that we have  $\tilde{\pmb{\theta}}_s(\mathcal{f}_h,\varepsilon)\leq \pmb{\theta}_s^{\mathrm{val}}(\mathcal{f}_h,\varepsilon)(16\log (h / \varepsilon) + 8)$ . we conclude the proof by noting that the value of  $\beta_{h}$  in algorithm 2 is simply the recursion in theorem f.1 with this upper bound substituted in, using the upper bound  $16\log (hk^{1 / 2} / \beta_h) + 8\leq 16\log (hk^{1 / 2}e) + 8\leq 24\log (hke)$  recursively to simplify.

# f.5 deferred proofs

proof of lemma f.3. let  $k$  and  $h$  be fixed. let  $\mathcal{h}^{(j)}$  denote the entire history for episode  $j$ . define a filtration

$$
\mathfrak {g} _ {j - 1} = \sigma (\mathcal {h} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \mathcal {h} ^ {(j - 1)}),
$$

with the convention  $\mathfrak{g}_0 = \emptyset$ . this filtration guarantees that  $(x_j^{(j,h)},a_j^{(j,h)})$  is  $\mathfrak{g}_j$ -measurable and  $\mathfrak{g}_{j - 1}\subseteq \mathfrak{g}_j$ . let  $f,f^{\prime}\in \mathcal{f}_h$  be fixed, and let  $x_{j} = (f(x_{j}^{(j,h)},a_{j}^{(j,h)}) - f^{\prime}(x_{j}^{(j,h)},a_{j}^{(j,h)}))^{2}$ . observe that  $|z_{j}|\leq h^{2}$ . applying lemma b.2 to the process  $(x_{j})$ , we are guaranteed that  $1 - \delta$ ,

$$
\sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k - 1} \mathbb {e} _ {j - 1} [ x _ {j} ] \leq 2 \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k - 1} \mathbb {e} _ {j - 1} [ x _ {j} ] + 8 h ^ {2} \log (2 \delta^ {- 1}).
$$

we have

$$
\sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k - 1} \mathbb {e} _ {j - 1} [ x _ {j} ] = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k - 1} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \pi^ {(j)}, a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \left[ (f (x _ {h}, a _ {h}) - f ^ {\prime} (x _ {h}, a _ {h})) ^ {2} \right] = \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {k, h} ^ {2}.
$$

by taking a union bound, we are guaranteed that

$$
\left\| f - f ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {k, h} ^ {2} \leq 2 \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} + 8 h ^ {2} \log \left(2 \mid \mathcal {f} _ {h} \mid \delta^ {- 1}\right) \tag {98}
$$

for all  $f, f' \in \mathcal{f}_h$ . we now deduce the result for the star hull by homogeneity. consider  $f'' \in \mathrm{star}(\mathcal{f}_h, f)$ , where  $f \in \mathcal{f}_h$ . we can write  $f'' = t(f' - f) + f$  for some  $f' \in \mathcal{f}_h$  and  $t \in [0,1]$ , so that  $f'' - f = t(f' - f)$ . (98) then implies that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| f - f ^ {\prime \prime} \| _ {k, h} ^ {2} = t ^ {2} \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {k, h} ^ {2} \leq 2 t ^ {2} \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} + t ^ {2} 8 h ^ {2} \log (2 | \mathcal {f} _ {h} | \delta^ {- 1}) \\ \leq 2 \| f - f ^ {\prime \prime} \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} + 8 h ^ {2} \log (2 | \mathcal {f} _ {h} | \delta^ {- 1}), \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $t \leq 1$ . this gives the result for  $k, h$  fixed. we union bound over all  $(k, h)$  pairs to get the final result.

proof of lemma f.4. for each  $i$ , we have

$$
\log \left(x _ {i + 1} / x _ {i}\right) = \log \left(1 + \left(\frac {x _ {i + 1}}{x _ {i}} - 1\right)\right) \geq \left(\frac {x _ {i + 1}}{x _ {i}} - 1\right) \cdot \frac {x _ {i}}{x _ {i + 1}} \geq \frac {1}{2} \left(\frac {x _ {i + 1}}{x _ {i}} - 1\right),
$$

where we have used the fact that  $\log (1 + y) \geq \frac{y}{y + 1}$  for  $y \geq 0$ , and that  $\frac{x_i}{x_{i+1}} \geq \frac{x_i}{x_i + 1} \geq \frac{1}{2}$ . it follows that

$$
\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \frac {x _ {i + 1} - x _ {i}}{x _ {i}} \leq 2 \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \log \left(x _ {i + 1} / x _ {i}\right) \leq 2 \log \left(x _ {n + 1} / x _ {1}\right).
$$

proof of lemma f.5. assume  $r / \varepsilon \geq e$ , otherwise  $\theta_{\mathcal{d};g^*}(\mathrm{star}(\mathcal{g},g^\star),\varepsilon)\leq e^2$ . observe that any  $g^{\prime}\in$ $\mathrm{star}(\mathcal{g},g^{\star})$  can be written as  $t(g - g^{\star}) + g^{\star})$  for  $g\in \mathcal{g}$ . consequently, we can write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} \sup  \left\{\left(g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)\right) ^ {2}: g \in \operatorname {s t a r} (\mathcal {g}, g ^ {\star}), \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} \left[ \left(g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq \varepsilon^ {2} \right\} \\ = \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} \sup  \bigl \{t ^ {2} (g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)) ^ {2}: t \in [ 0, 1 ], g \in \mathcal {g}, t ^ {2} \cdot \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} [ (g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)) ^ {2} ] \leq \varepsilon^ {2} \bigr \} \\ \end{array}
$$

let  $n = \lceil \log (r / \varepsilon) \rceil$ , and let  $a_0 = 1$  and  $a_i = e^{-i}$  for  $i \in [n]$ . then we can bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} \sup  \left\{t ^ {2} (g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)) ^ {2}: t \in [ 0, 1 ], g \in \mathcal {g}, t ^ {2} \cdot \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} \left[ (g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)) ^ {2} \right] \leq \varepsilon^ {2} \right\} \\ \leq \operatorname {\mathbb {e}} _ {\mathcal {p}} \sup  _ {i \in [ n ]} \sup  \left\{t ^ {2} (g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)) ^ {2}: t \in [ a _ {i}, a _ {i - 1} ], g \in \mathcal {g}, t ^ {2} \cdot \operatorname {\mathbb {e}} _ {\mathcal {p}} [ (g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)) ^ {2} ] \leq \varepsilon^ {2} \right\} + \varepsilon^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

since any  $t \notin [a_n, 1]$  has  $|t| \leq \varepsilon / r$ . we further upper bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} \sup  _ {i \in [ n ]} \sup  \left\{t ^ {2} (g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)) ^ {2}: t \in [ a _ {i}, a _ {i - 1} ], g \in \mathcal {g}, t ^ {2} \cdot \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} [ (g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)) ^ {2} ] \leq \varepsilon^ {2} \right\} \\ \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} \sup  \left\{t ^ {2} (g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)) ^ {2}: t \in [ a _ {i}, a _ {i - 1} ], g \in \mathcal {g}, t ^ {2} \cdot \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} [ (g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)) ^ {2} ] \leq \varepsilon^ {2} \right\} \\ \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} a _ {i - 1} ^ {2} \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} \sup  \left\{\left(g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)\right) ^ {2}: g \in \mathcal {g}, \cdot \mathbb {e} _ {\mathcal {p}} \left[ \left(g (z) - g ^ {\star} (z)\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq \varepsilon^ {2} / a _ {i} ^ {2} \right\} \\ \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \frac {a _ {i - 1} ^ {2}}{a _ {i} ^ {2}} \varepsilon^ {2} \boldsymbol {\theta} _ {\mathcal {d}; g ^ {*}} (\mathcal {g}, \varepsilon) \\ \leq e ^ {2} n \cdot \varepsilon^ {2} \cdot \theta_ {\mathcal {d}; g ^ {\star}} (\mathcal {g}, \varepsilon). \\ \end{array}
$$

where we have used that  $a_{i - 1} / a_i\leq e$  for all  $i$ . finally, since  $r / \varepsilon \geq 1$ , we have  $n\leq 2\log (r / \varepsilon)$ . since  $\pmb{\theta}_{\mathcal{d};g^{\star}}(\mathcal{g},\varepsilon)\geq 1$ , by combining both cases we have  $\pmb{\theta}_{\mathcal{d};g^{\star}}(\mathrm{star}(\mathcal{g},g^{\star}),\varepsilon)\leq e^{2}(2\log (r / \varepsilon) + 1)\pmb{\theta}_{\mathcal{d};g^{\star}}(\mathcal{g},\varepsilon)$ .

# g proof of theorem f.1

# g.1 preliminaries

recall that at round  $k$ , for each layer  $h$ , we solve

$$
\widehat{f}_{h}^{(k)} = \operatorname *{arg  min}_{f\in \mathcal{f}_{h}}\sum_{j <   k}\bigl(f(x_{h}^{(j,h)},a_{h}^{(j,h)}) - \bigl(r_{h}^{(j,h)} + \overline{\mathbf{v}}_{h + 1}^{(k)}(x_{h + 1}^{(j,h)})\bigr)\bigr)^{2},
$$

where for each round  $j$ ,  $x_{h}^{(j,h)}, a_{h}^{(j,h)}, x_{h+1}^{(j,h)}$  are obtained by rolling in until time  $h$  with  $\pi^{(j)}$ , then sampling  $a_{h}$  uniformly at random.

this proof is inductive. let  $1 \leq h \leq h - 1$  be fixed. suppose we can guarantee that for layer  $h + 1$ , with probability at least  $1 - \delta_{h + 1}$ , we have

$$
\left\| \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)}} \leq \beta_ {h + 1},
$$

so that  $\bar{f}_h^{(k)}\in \widehat{\mathcal{f}}_{h + 1}^{(k)} = \big\{f\in \mathrm{star}(\mathcal{f}_{h + 1},\widehat{f}_{h + 1}^{(k)}):\| f - \widehat{f}_{h + 1}^{(k)}\|_{\mathcal{z}_{h + 1}^{(k)}}\leq \beta_{h + 1}\big\}$ . our goal is to figure out, based on this inductive hypothesis, what are admissible values for  $\beta_h$  and  $\delta_h$  for layer  $h$ . we handle the base case for layer  $h$ , which is straightforward, at the end of the proof.

# g.2 initial bound on least squares error

define

$$
l _ {h} ^ {(k)} (f) = \sum_ {j <   k} \left(f \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - \left(r _ {h} ^ {(j, h)} + \overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\right) ^ {2}\right).
$$

then using strong convexity of the square loss (following the usual basic inequality argument for least squares), we have

$$
0 \geq l _ {h} ^ {(k)} (\widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)}) - l _ {h} ^ {(k)} (\bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)}) \geq d _ {f} l _ {h} ^ {(k)} (\bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)}) [ \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} ] + \left\| \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2}.
$$

now note that we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} d _ {f} l _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(\bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)}\right) \left[ \hat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right] \\ = 2 \sum_ {j <   k} \left(\bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - \left(r _ {h} ^ {(j, h)} + \overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\right)\right) \left(\hat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

abbreviating  $z_{h}^{(j,h)} = (x_{h}^{(j,h)},a_{h}^{(j,h)})$ , we can use this to rewrite the previous expression as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left| \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right| \right| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \\ \leq 4 \sum_ {j <   k} \left(\bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - \left(r _ {h} ^ {(j, h)} + \overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\right)\right) \left(\widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\right) - \left\| \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $\widehat{f}_h^{(k)},\bar{f}_h^{(k)}\in \mathcal{f}_h$  , if we define  $\mathcal{g}_h = \mathcal{f}_h - \mathcal{f}_h$  , we can further upper bound as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} (99) \\ \leq \sup  _ {g \in \mathcal {g} _ {h}} \left\{4 \sum_ {j <   k} \left(\bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - \left(r _ {h} ^ {(j, h)} + \overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\right)\right) g \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - \| g \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \right\} \\ = \sup  _ {g \in \mathcal {g} _ {h}} \left\{ \right.4 \sum_ {j <   k} \left( \right.\left[ p _ {h} ^ {\star} \overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right]\left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - \overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)} + \zeta_ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) g \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - \sum_ {j <   k} g ^ {2} \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\left. \right\}, (100) \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\zeta_h^{(j,h)}\coloneqq f^{\star}(z_h^{(j,h)}) - r_h^{(j,h)}$

now, recall that

$$
\overline{\mathbf{v}}^{(k)}_{h + 1}(x) = \max_{a}\sup_{f\in \widehat{\mathcal{f}}^{(k)}_{h + 1}}f(x,a).
$$

let us define another set,

$$
\overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h} ^ {(k)} (\varepsilon) = \left\{f \in \operatorname {s t a r} \left(\mathcal {f} _ {h}, \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)}\right): \left\| f - \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \leq \varepsilon \right\}.
$$

let  $\tilde{\beta}_{h + 1}\geq \beta_{h + 1}$  be a free parameter, and define

$$
\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}^{(k)}_{h + 1}(x,a) = \sup_{f\in \overline{\mathcal{f}}^{(k)}_{h + 1}(\tilde{\beta}^{2}_{h + 1})}f(x,a)
$$

and  $\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{h + 1}^{(k)}(x) = \max_a\widetilde{\mathbf{q}}_{h + 1}^{(k)}(x,a)$ . then we can write

$$
\overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x) = \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x) + \xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x),
$$

where

$$
\left| \xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x) \right| \leq \max  _ {a} \left| \widetilde {\mathbf {q}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x, a) - \overline {{\mathbf {q}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x, a) \right|.
$$

in particular, returning to (100), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left| \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right| \right| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \\ \leq \sup  _ {g \in \mathcal {g} _ {h}} \left\{4 \sum_ {j <   k} \left(\left[ p _ {h} ^ {\star} \overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right] \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - \overline {{\mathbf {v}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}\right) + \zeta_ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) g \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - \sum_ {j <   k} g ^ {2} \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) \right\}. \\ \leq \sup  _ {g \in \mathcal {g} _ {h}} \left\{4 \sum_ {j <   k} \big (\big [ p _ {h} ^ {\star} \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \big ] (z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}) - \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}) + \zeta_ {h} ^ {(j, h)} + \big [ p _ {h} ^ {\star} \xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \big ] (z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}) - \xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}) \big) g (z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}) - \sum_ {j <   k} g ^ {2} (z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}) \right\}. \\ \end{array}
$$

using the am-gm inequality, we have

$$
\xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}\right) \cdot g \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) \leq \frac {3}{2} \left(\xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\right) ^ {2} + \frac {1}{3} g ^ {2} \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right),
$$

and likewise  $\left[p_h^\star \xi_{h + 1}^{(k)}\right](z_h^{(j,h)})\cdot g(z_h^{(j,h)})\leq \frac{3}{2}\bigl (\bigl [p_h^\star \xi_{h + 1}^{(k)}\bigr ](z_h^{(j,h)})\bigr)^2 +\frac{1}{3} g^2 (z_h^{(j,h)})$  , so we can further upper bound the process above by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underbrace {\sup  _ {g \in \mathcal {g} _ {h}} \left\{4 \sum_ {j <   k} \left(\left[ p _ {h} ^ {\star} \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right] \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j , h)}\right) - \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j , h)}\right) + \zeta_ {h} ^ {(j , h)}\right) \cdot g \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j , h)}\right) - \frac {1}{3} \sum_ {j <   k} g ^ {2} \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j , h)}\right) \right\}} _ {=: \mathsf {o p} _ {h}} \\ + \underbrace {\frac {3}{2} \sum_ {j <   k} (\xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j , h)})) ^ {2} + \left(\left[ p _ {h} ^ {*} \xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right] (z _ {h} ^ {(j , h)})\right) ^ {2}} _ {=: \mathrm {e t} _ {h}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\mathsf{op}_h$  is an offset process and  $\mathsf{et}_h$  is an error term.

# g.3 bounding the offset process

recall that

$$
\widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x) = \max  _ {a} \sup  \left\{f (x, a) \mid f \in \operatorname {s t a r} \left(\mathcal {f} _ {h + 1}, \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)}\right), \left\| f - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \leq \tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2} \right\}.
$$

hence, if we define a set

$$
\mathcal{v}_{h + 1}^{(k)} = \left\{x\mapsto \max_{a}\sup_{f\in \operatorname {star}(\mathcal{f}_{h + 1},f^{\prime}): \| f - f^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{l}}^{2}\leq \tilde{\beta}_{h + 1}^{2}}f(x,a)\mid f^{\prime}\in \mathcal{f}_{h + 1},\mathcal{l}\subseteq \mathcal{s}_{h + 1}^{k - 1}\right\} ,
$$

we have  $\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{h + 1}^{(k)}\in \mathcal{v}_{h + 1}^{(k)}$ . note that  $\mathcal{v}_{h + 1}^{(k)}$  is not a random variable, which is critical for the analysis. we can bound the size of  $\mathcal{v}_{h + 1}^{(k)}$  as follows. first, observe that each function in  $\mathcal{v}_{h + 1}^{(k)}$  is uniquely defined by the choice of the center  $f^{\prime}$  and the set  $\mathcal{l}\subseteq s_{h + 1}^{k - 1}$ . moreover, for any two sets  $\mathcal{l},\mathcal{l}'$  that are equivalent up to permutation, we have  $\| f\|_{\mathcal{l}} = \| f\|_{\mathcal{l}'}$  for all  $f$ , meaning that the norm in the constraint is determined only by the multiset of states in  $\mathcal{l}$ . by the usual stars-and-bars counting argument, there are only  $\left(\binom{k}{s-1}\right)=\binom{k+s-2}{s-1}\leq\left(\frac{e(k+s-2)}{s-1}\right)^s\leq(2ek)^s$  possible such choices (assuming  $s > 1$ , if not there is clearly at most 1 such choice). hence, altogether, we have

$$
\left| \mathcal {v} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right| \leq (2 e k) ^ {s} \left| \mathcal {f} _ {h + 1} \right|. \tag {101}
$$

we move to the upper bound

$$
\mathsf{op}_{h}\leq \sup_{g\in \mathcal{g}_{h}}\sup_{v\in \mathcal{v}^{(k)}_{h + 1}}\left\{4\sum_{j <   k}\bigl([p_{h}^{\star}v](x_{h}^{(j,h)},a_{h}^{(j,h)}) - v(x_{h + 1}^{(j,h)} + \zeta_{h}^{(j,h)})\bigr)\cdot g(z_{h}^{(j,h)}) - \frac{1}{3}\sum_{j <   k}g^{2}(z_{h}^{(j,h)})\right\} .
$$

we now appeal to the following lemma.

lemma g.1. let  $k$  and  $h$  be fixed. for any function classes  $\mathcal{g} \subseteq ((\mathcal{x}_h \times \mathcal{a}) \to [-h, +h])$  and  $\mathcal{v} \subseteq (\mathcal{x}_{h+1} \to [0, h])$  and any constant  $c \in (0, 1]$ , with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ ,

$$
\sup  _ {g \in \mathcal {g}} \sup  _ {v \in \mathcal {v}} \left\{ \right.\sum_ {j <   k} \left( \right.[ p _ {h} ^ {\star} v ] \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - v \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)} + \zeta_ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) \cdot g \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - c \sum_ {j <   k} g ^ {2} \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\left. \right\} \leq \frac {4 h ^ {2} \log \left(\left| \mathcal {g} \right|\left| \mathcal {v} \right| \delta^ {- 1}\right)}{c}. \tag {102}
$$

using lemma g.1 along with the bound from (101), we have that with probability at least  $1 - \delta$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathrm {o p} _ {h} \leq 1 9 2 h ^ {2} \log \left(\left| \mathcal {g} _ {h} \right| \left| \mathcal {v} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right| \delta^ {- 1}\right) \\ \leq 1 9 2 h ^ {2} \log \left(\left| \mathcal {f} _ {h} \right| ^ {2} \left| \mathcal {f} _ {h + 1} \right| (2 e k) ^ {s} \delta^ {- 1}\right) \\ \leq 5 7 6 h ^ {2} \left(s \log (2 e k) + \log \left(f _ {\max } \delta^ {- 1}\right)\right). \tag {103} \\ \end{array}
$$

# g.4 bounding the approximation error

by jensen's inequality, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathsf {e t} _ {h} = \frac {3}{2} \sum_ {j <   k} \left(\xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\right) ^ {2} + \left(\left[ p _ {h} ^ {\star} \xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right] \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\right) ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {3}{2} \sum_ {j <   k} (\xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})) ^ {2} + \mathbb {e} \big [ (\xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1})) ^ {2} \mid x _ {h} = x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} = a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)} \big ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

now, recall that for any  $x$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x) \right| \leq \max  _ {a} \left| \widetilde {\mathbf {q}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x, a) - \overline {{\mathbf {q}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x, a) \right| \\ = \max  _ {a} \left| \sup  _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(\tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2}\right)} f (x, a) - \sup  _ {f \in \widehat {\mathcal {f}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)}} f (x, a) \right|. \\ \end{array}
$$

to relate the two terms in the absolute value, we appeal to a uniform concentration lemma.

lemma g.2. let  $k$ ,  $h$ , and  $\varepsilon \in (0,1)$  be fixed. with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ , we have that for all  $f \in \mathcal{f}_h$  and  $f' \in \mathrm{star}(\mathcal{f}_h, f)$ ,

$$
(1 - \varepsilon) \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h} \leq \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \leq (1 + \varepsilon) \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h} \tag {104}
$$

where  $c_{\varepsilon ,\delta ;h}\leq \frac{2h^2\log(2|\mathcal{f}_h|\delta^{-1})}{\varepsilon}.$

fix  $\varepsilon$ ,  $\delta$  to be chosen later. lemma g.2 implies that with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ ,

$$
\bar {\mathcal {f}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left((1 - \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}\right) \subseteq \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \subseteq \bar {\mathcal {f}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left((1 + \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}\right), \tag {105}
$$

where we assume for now that  $\varepsilon$  is chosen such that  $(1 - \varepsilon)\beta_{h + 1}^{2} - c_{\varepsilon ,\delta ;h + 1} > 0$

let us set  $\tilde{\beta}_{h + 1}^2 = (1 + \varepsilon)\beta_{h + 1}^2 +c_{\varepsilon ,\delta ;h + 1}$ . then, conditioned on the event above, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x) \right| \leq \max  _ {a} \left| \sup  _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \left(\tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2}\right)} f (x, a) - \sup  _ {f \in \hat {\mathcal {f}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)}} f (x, a) \right| \\ = \max  _ {a} \left\{\sup  _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (\tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2})} f (x, a) - \sup  _ {f \in \widehat {\mathcal {f}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)}} f (x, a) \right\}, \\ \end{array}
$$

since the nesting property in (105) implies that  $\sup_{f\in \overline{\mathcal{f}}^{(k)}_{h + 1}(\tilde{\beta}_{h + 1}^2)}f(x,a)$  is always the larger of the two terms. using the other direction of the nesting property, we can further upper bound by

$$
\left| \xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x) \right| \leq \max  _ {a} \left\{\sup  _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} ((1 + \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1})} f (x, a) - \sup  _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} ((1 - \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1})} f (x, a) \right\}.
$$

let  $x$  and  $a$  be fixed, and consider a function  $f^{\star}$  that achieves the value

$$
\sup_{\substack{f\in \overline{\mathcal{f}}^{(k)}_{h + 1}((1 + \varepsilon)\beta^{2}_{h + 1} + c_{\varepsilon ,\delta ;h + 1})\\ }}f(x,a).
$$

if the maximum is not achieved, we can simply consider a sequence of functions approaching the supremum, but we omit the details.

now, observe that since  $\mathrm{star}(\mathcal{f}_{h + 1},\widehat{f}_{h + 1}^{(k)})$  contains any function of the form  $t(f - \widehat{f}_{h + 1}^{(k)}) + \widehat{f}_{h + 1}^{(k)}$  for  $f\in \mathcal{f}_{h + 1}$ $t\in [0,1]$  by definition, we have

$$
\tilde {f} := \left(\frac {(1 - \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{(1 + \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \cdot (f ^ {\star} - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)}) + \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} ((1 - \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}),
$$

where we have used that  $(1 - \varepsilon)\beta_{h + 1}^{2} - c_{\varepsilon ,\delta ;h + 1}\geq 0$  . this means that

$$
\sup  \quad f (x, a) - \quad \sup  \quad f (x, a)
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} ((1 + \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}) \quad f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} ((1 - \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}) \\ \leq f ^ {\star} (x, a) - \tilde {f} (x, a) \\ = \left(1 - \left(\frac {(1 - \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{(1 + \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}\right) ^ {1 / 2}\right) \left(f ^ {\star} (x, a) - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x, a)\right) \\ = \left(1 - \left(\frac {(1 - \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{(1 + \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}\right) ^ {1 / 2}\right) \sup  _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} ((1 + \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1})} \left\{f (x, a) - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x, a) \right\} \\ \end{array}
$$

we also have

$$
\left(1 - \left(\frac {(1 - \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{(1 + \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}\right) ^ {1 / 2}\right) ^ {2} \leq 1 - \frac {(1 - \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{(1 + \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}} \leq 2 \left(\varepsilon + \frac {c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{\beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2}}\right).
$$

altogether, this allows us to bound

$$
\sum_ {j <   k} (\xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})) ^ {2} \leq 2 \left(\varepsilon + \frac {c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{\beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2}}\right) \sum_ {j <   k} \max  _ {a} \sup  _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (\tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2})} \left(f (x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}, a) - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}, a)\right) ^ {2}
$$

now, consider the function

$$
w _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x) := \max _ {a} \sup _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (\tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2})} \left(f (x, a) - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x, a)\right) ^ {2}.
$$

as in the analysis for  $\mathsf{op}_h$ , we argue that this function belongs to a relatively small class. in particular, we have

$$
w_{h + 1}^{(k)}\in \mathcal{w}_{h + 1}^{(k)}:= \left\{x\mapsto \max_{a}\max_{f\in \mathrm{star}(\mathcal{f}_{h + 1},f^{\prime}):\| f - f^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{l}}^{2}\leq \tilde{\beta}_{h + 1}^{2}}\big(f(x,a) - f^{\prime}(x,a)\big)^{2}\mid f^{\prime}\in \mathcal{f}_{h + 1},\mathcal{l}\subseteq \mathcal{s}_{h + 1}^{k - 1}\right\} .
$$

through the same counting argument as in the analysis of  $\mathsf{op}_h$ , we have  $\left|\mathcal{w}_{h + 1}^{(k)}\right| \leq (2ek)^s |\mathcal{f}_{h + 1}|$ . we use this to relate

$$
\sum_ {j <   k} (\xi_ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})) ^ {2} = \sum_ {j <   k} w _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})
$$

to a conditional-expected variant of the same quantity via a uniform concentration bound.

lemma g.3. with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ , for all  $w \in \mathcal{w}_{h+1}^{(k)}$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {j <   k} w (x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}) \leq \frac {3}{2} \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})} [ w (x _ {h + 1}) ] + 4 h ^ {2} \big (s \log (2 e k) + \log (2 | \mathcal {f} _ {h + 1} | \delta^ {- 1}) \big)
$$

and

$$
\sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} \big [ w (x _ {h + 1}) \mid x _ {h} = x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} = a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)} \big ] \leq 3 \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})} [ w (x _ {h + 1}) ] + 1 6 h ^ {2} \big (s \log (2 e k) + \log (2 | \mathcal {f} _ {h + 1} | \delta^ {- 1}) \big).
$$

conditioned on the event in lemma g.3, we have

$$
\mathsf {e t} _ {h} \leq 2 8 \bigg (\varepsilon + \frac {c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{\beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2}} \bigg) \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})} \big [ w _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1}) \big ] + 6 0 \bigg (\varepsilon + \frac {c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{\beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2}} \bigg) h ^ {2} \big (s \log (2 e k) + \log (2 | \mathcal {f} _ {h + 1} | \delta^ {- 1}) \big).
$$

we can further upper bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})} \big [ w _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1}) \big ] = \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})} \max _ {a} \sup _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (\widetilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2})} (f (x _ {h + 1}, a) - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1}) ^ {2} \\ \leq a\sum_{j <   k}\mathbb{e}_{x_{h + 1}\sim \psi \left(s_{h + 1}^{(j,h)}\right)}\mathbb{e}_{a_{h + 1}\sim \pi_{\mathrm{unif}}}\sup_{f\in \overline{\mathcal{f}}_{h + 1}^{(k)}(\tilde{\beta}_{h + 1}^{2})}(f(x_{h + 1},a_{h + 1}) - \widehat{f}_{h + 1}^{(k)}(x_{h + 1},a_{h + 1}))^{2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

now, recall that each  $f\in \overline{\mathcal{f}}_{h + 1}^{(k)}(\tilde{\beta}_{h + 1}^2)$  has

$$
\left\| f - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} = \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h + 1} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} (f (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1}) - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1})) ^ {2} \leq \tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2}
$$

by definition. letting  $n_h^{(k)}(s) = |\{j < k : s^{(j,h)} = s\}|$  for each  $s \in s_h$ , this implies

$$
\sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h + 1}} n _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (s) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h + 1} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \big (f (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1}) - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1}) \big) ^ {2} \leq \tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2},
$$

so, in particular,

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h + 1} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} (f (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1}) - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1})) ^ {2} \leq \frac {\widetilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2}}{n _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (s)}
$$

for all  $s\in s_{h + 1}$

we can similarly upper bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h + 1} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \sup  _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (\tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2})} \big (f (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1}) - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1}) \big) ^ {2} \\ = \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h + 1}} n _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (s) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h + 1} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \sup  _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (\tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2})} (f (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1}) - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1})) ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

recall that for each latent state  $s \in s_h$  and  $f^{\star} \in \mathcal{f}_h$ , we define the disagreement coefficient as

$$
\check {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {s} (\varepsilon_ {0}) = \sup  _ {f ^ {\star} \in \mathcal {f} _ {h}} \sup  _ {\varepsilon > \varepsilon_ {0}} \frac {\mathbb {e} _ {x \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \sup  \left\{(f (x , a) - f ^ {\star} (x , a)) ^ {2} : f \in \operatorname {s t a r} (\mathcal {f} _ {h} , f ^ {\star}) , \| f - f ^ {\star} \| _ {s} ^ {2} \leq \varepsilon^ {2} \right\}}{\varepsilon^ {2}}
$$

it follows from the discussion above that we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h + 1}} n _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (s) \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s)} \mathbb {e} _ {a _ {h + 1} \sim \pi_ {\mathrm {u n i f}}} \sup  _ {f \in \overline {{\mathcal {f}}} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (\tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2})} \big (f \big (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1} \big) - \widehat {f} _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (x _ {h + 1}, a _ {h + 1}) \big) ^ {2} \\ \leq \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h + 1}} n _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (s) \cdot \check {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {s} \left(\tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} / \left(n _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (s)\right) ^ {1 / 2}\right) \frac {\tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2}}{n _ {h + 1} ^ {(k)} (s)} \\ \leq \tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2} \sum_ {s \in \mathcal {s} _ {h + 1}} \check {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {s} \left(\beta_ {h + 1} / k ^ {1 / 2}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we have used that  $\tilde{\pmb{\theta}}$  is decreasing in  $\varepsilon$ . recalling the definition  $\tilde{\pmb{\theta}}_{h + 1}(\varepsilon) = \sum_{s\in s_{h + 1}}\tilde{\pmb{\theta}}_s(\varepsilon)$ , we are guaranteed that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathsf {e t} _ {h} \leq 2 8 a \breve {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {h + 1} (\beta_ {h + 1} k ^ {- 1 / 2}) \left(\varepsilon + \frac {c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{\beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2}}\right) \tilde {\beta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2} + 6 0 \left(\varepsilon + \frac {c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{\beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2}}\right) h ^ {2} \big (s \log (2 e k) + \log (2 | \mathcal {f} _ {h + 1} | \delta^ {- 1}) \big) \\ \leq 28a\breve{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{h + 1}(\beta_{h + 1}k^{-1 / 2})\bigg(2\varepsilon \beta_{h + 1}^{2} + 3c_{\varepsilon ,\delta ;h + 1} + \frac{c_{\varepsilon,\delta;h + 1}^{2}}{\beta_{h + 1}^{2}}\bigg) + 60\bigg(\varepsilon +\frac{c_{\varepsilon,\delta;h + 1}}{\beta_{h + 1}^{2}}\bigg)h^{2}\big(s\log (2ek) + \log (2|\mathcal{f}_{h + 1}| \delta^{-1})\big). \\ \end{array}
$$

# g.5 putting everything together

combining the bounds on  $\mathsf{op}_h$  and  $\mathsf{et}_h$  and taking a union bound, we are guaranteed that with probability at least  $1 - 3\delta - \delta_{h + 1}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \leq 2 8 a \breve {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {h + 1} (\hat {\beta} _ {h + 1}) \left(2 \varepsilon \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + 3 c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1} + \frac {c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1} ^ {2}}{\beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2}}\right) \\ + 6 0 \left(\varepsilon + \frac {c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1}}{\beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2}}\right) h ^ {2} \left(s \log (2 e k) + \log (2 | \mathcal {f} _ {h + 1} | \delta^ {- 1})\right) \\ + 5 7 6 h ^ {2} \left(s \log (2 e k) + \log \left(f _ {\max } \delta^ {- 1}\right)\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we define  $\hat{\beta}_{h + 1} = \beta_{h + 1}k^{-1 / 2}$ . this bound holds for any  $\varepsilon \in (0,1)$  chosen a-priori, so long as  $(1 - \varepsilon)\beta_{h + 1}^2 -c_{\varepsilon ,\delta ;h + 1} > 0$ . we now make an appropriate choice. recall that

$$
c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1} \leq \frac {2 h ^ {2} \log (2 | \mathcal {f} _ {h + 1} | \delta^ {- 1})}{\varepsilon}.
$$

we choose

$$
\varepsilon = \sqrt {\frac {2 h ^ {2} \log (2 f _ {\mathrm {m a x}} \delta^ {- 1})}{\beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2}}}.
$$

we have  $\varepsilon \leq 1$  as long as  $\beta_{h + 1}^2\geq 2h^2\log (2f_{\max}\delta^{-1})$ . moreover,

$$
(1 - \varepsilon) \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h + 1} \geq \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} - 2 \beta_ {h + 1} \sqrt {2 h ^ {2} \log (2 f _ {\max} \delta^ {- 1})},
$$

so this quantity is non-negative as long  $\beta_{h + 1}^2\geq 8h^2\log (2f_{\max}\delta^{-1})$

assume for now that this constraint holds. then we can bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \leq 2 8 a \breve {\theta} _ {h + 1} (\hat {\beta} _ {h + 1}) \left(5 \beta_ {h + 1} \sqrt {2 h ^ {2} \log (2 f _ {\max } \delta^ {- 1})} + 2 h ^ {2} \log (2 f _ {\max } \delta^ {- 1})\right) \\ + 1 2 0 h ^ {2} \left(s \log (2 e k) + \log \left(2 f _ {\max } \delta^ {- 1}\right)\right) \\ + 5 7 6 h ^ {2} \left(s \log (2 e k) + \log \left(f _ {\max } \delta^ {- 1}\right)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

furthermore, using the am-gm inequality, we have

$$
2 8 a \breve {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {h + 1} (\hat {\beta} _ {h + 1}) \left(5 \beta_ {h + 1} \sqrt {2 h ^ {2} \log (2 f _ {\max} \delta^ {- 1})}\right) \leq \frac {1}{2} \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + 1 4 0 ^ {2} a ^ {2} \breve {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {h + 1} ^ {2} (\hat {\beta} _ {h + 1}) h ^ {2} \log (2 f _ {\max} \delta^ {- 1}).
$$

using this, we can (rather coarsely) simplify the error bound to

$$
\left\| \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{2} \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + 1 5 0 ^ {2} h ^ {2} a ^ {2} \check {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {h + 1} ^ {2} (\hat {\beta} _ {h + 1}) \log (2 f _ {\max } \delta^ {- 1}) + 7 0 0 h ^ {2} s \log (2 e k).
$$

the critical detail here is that the constant in front of  $\beta_{h + 1}^2$  is no larger than 1, so there is no exponential blowup as we propagate this constraint backward.

overall, we get that any sequences  $(\delta_h), (\beta_h)$  are admissible as long for all  $1 \leq h \leq h - 1$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \beta_ {h} ^ {2} \geq \frac {1}{2} \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + 1 5 0 ^ {2} h ^ {2} a ^ {2} \check {\boldsymbol {\theta}} _ {h + 1} ^ {2} (\hat {\beta} _ {h + 1}) \log (2 f _ {\max } \delta^ {- 1}) + 7 0 0 h ^ {2} s \log (2 e k), \\ \beta_ {h} ^ {2} \geq 8 h ^ {2} \log \left(2 f _ {\max} \delta^ {- 1}\right), \\ \delta_ {h} \geq 3 \delta + \delta_ {h + 1}, \\ \end{array}
$$

for any  $\delta \in (0,1)$  chosen a-priori.

for the base case, we observe that since  $\overline{\mathbf{v}}_{h + 1}^{(k)}(x) = \mathbf{v}_{h + 1}^{\star}(x) = 0$  for all  $k$ , we have that for layer  $h$ ,

$$
\left\| \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \leq \sup  _ {g \in \mathcal {g} _ {h}} \left\{4 \sum_ {j <   k} \delta_ {h} ^ {(j, h)} \cdot g (z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}) - \frac {1}{3} \sum_ {j <   k} g ^ {2} (z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}) \right\}.
$$

by lemma g.1, this implies

$$
\left\| \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \leq 4 0 0 h ^ {2} \log \left(f _ {\max } \delta^ {- 1}\right)
$$

with probability at least  $1 - \delta$

we conclude that the following sequence is admissible:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \delta_ {h - 1} = \delta \\ \beta_ {h} ^ {2} = 4 0 0 h ^ {2} \log (f _ {\mathrm {m a x}} \delta^ {- 1}) \\ \delta_ {h} = 3 \delta + \delta_ {h + 1} \\ \beta_ {h} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{2} \beta_ {h + 1} ^ {2} + 1 5 0 ^ {2} h ^ {2} a ^ {2} \check {\theta} _ {h + 1} ^ {2} (\beta_ {h + 1} k ^ {- 1 / 2}) \log (2 f _ {\max} \delta^ {- 1}) + 7 0 0 h ^ {2} s \log (2 e k). \\ \end{array}
$$

in particular, this guarantees that with probability at least  $1 - \delta_{1} \geq 1 - 3h\delta$ , we have

$$
\left\| \widehat {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} - \bar {f} _ {h} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \leq \beta_ {h} ^ {2}
$$

for all  $h$  within round  $k$ . by union bound, the same holds for all  $k$  with probability at least  $1 - 3hk\delta$ .

# g.6 deferred proofs

proof of lemma g.1. define a filtration

$$
\mathfrak {g} _ {j - 1} = \sigma (\mathcal {h} ^ {(1)}, \ldots , \mathcal {h} ^ {(j - 1)}, (s _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}, x _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}, r _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}), \ldots , (s _ {h - 1} ^ {(j, h)}, x _ {h - 1} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h - 1} ^ {(j, h)}, r _ {h - 1} ^ {(j, h)}), (s _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)})).
$$

let  $g \in \mathcal{g}$ ,  $v \in \mathcal{v}$  be fixed, and let  $z_{j} = \left([p_{h}^{\star}v](x_{h}^{(j,h)},a_{h}^{(j,h)}) - v(x_{h + 1}^{(j,h)}) + \zeta_{h}^{(j,h)})\right) \cdot g(z_{h}^{(j,h)})$ . observe that  $z_{j}$  is  $\mathfrak{s}_j$ -measurable and is a martingale difference sequence, since

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ z _ {j} \mid \mathfrak {g} _ {j - 1} \right] = \mathbb {e} \left[ \left(\left[ p _ {h} ^ {\star} v \right] \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - v \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}\right) + f ^ {\star} \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - r _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) \cdot g \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) \mid x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)} \right] \\ = \left([ p _ {h} ^ {\star} v ] \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - [ p _ {h} ^ {\star} v ] \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) + f ^ {\star} \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) - f ^ {\star} \left(x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right)\right) \cdot g \left(z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right) \\ = 0. \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $|z_{j}| \leq h(h + 1)$ , we have by lemma b.1 that for any  $\eta \leq 1 / h(h + 1)$ , with probability at least  $1 - \delta$

$$
\sum_ {j <   k} z _ {j} \leq \eta \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {j - 1} \left[ z _ {j} ^ {2} \right] + \frac {\log (\delta^ {- 1})}{\eta}.
$$

moreover

$$
\sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {j - 1} \left[ z _ {j} ^ {2} \right] \leq (h + 1) ^ {2} \sum_ {j <   k} g ^ {2} \left(z ^ {(j, h)}\right).
$$

hence, by choosing  $\eta = \frac{c}{(h + 1)^2}$ , we are guaranteed that with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ ,

$$
\sum_ {j <   k} \big ([ p _ {h} ^ {\star} v ] (x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}) - v (x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}) + \zeta_ {h} ^ {(j, h)} \big) \cdot g (z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}) - c \sum_ {j <   k} g ^ {2} (z _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}) \leq \frac {(h + 1) ^ {2} \log (\delta^ {- 1})}{c}.
$$

the result now follows by taking a union bound over all  $g \in \mathcal{g}$  and  $v \in \mathcal{v}$ .

proof of lemma g.2. let  $k$  and  $h$  be fixed. let  $\mathcal{h}^{(j)}$  denote the entire history for episode  $j$ . define a filtration

$$
\mathfrak {g} _ {j - 1} = \sigma (\mathcal {h} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \mathcal {h} ^ {(j - 1)}, (s _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}, x _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}), \dots , (s _ {h - 1} ^ {(j, h)}, x _ {h - 1} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h - 1} ^ {(j, h)}), s _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}),
$$

with the convention  $\mathfrak{g}_0 = \sigma((s_1^{(j,h)}, x_1^{(j,h)}, a_1^{(j,h)}), \ldots, (s_{h-1}^{(j,h)}, x_{h-1}^{(j,h)}, a_{h-1}^{(j,h)}), s_h^{(j,h)})$ . this filtration guarantees that  $(x_j^{(j,h)}, a_j^{(j,h)})$  is  $\mathfrak{g}_j$ -measurable and  $\mathfrak{g}_{j-1} \subseteq \mathfrak{g}_j$ .

let  $f, f' \in \mathcal{f}_h$  be fixed, and let  $x_j = (f(x_j^{(j,h)}, a_j^{(j,h)})) - f'(x_j^{(j,h)}, a_j^{(j,h)}))^2$  and  $z_j = x_j - \mathbb{e}_{j-1}[x_j]$ , where  $\mathbb{e}_{j-1}[\cdot] = \mathbb{e}[\cdot \mid \mathfrak{s}_{j-1}]$ . observe that  $|z_j| \leq h^2$ . applying lemma b.1 to both the process  $(z_j)$  and  $(-z_j)$ , we are guaranteed that for any  $\lambda \in [0,1/h^2]$ , with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k - 1} x _ {j} - \mathbb {e} _ {j - 1} [ x _ {j} ] \right| \leq \lambda \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k - 1} \mathbb {e} _ {j - 1} \left[ x _ {j} ^ {2} \right] + \frac {\log (2 \delta^ {- 1})}{\lambda} \\ \leq \lambda h ^ {2} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k - 1} \mathbb {e} _ {j - 1} \left[ x _ {j} \right] + \frac {\log (2 \delta^ {- 1})}{\lambda}. \\ \end{array}
$$

we have

$$
\sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k - 1} \mathbb {e} _ {j - 1} [ x _ {j} ] = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {k - 1} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h} \sim \psi \left(s _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}\right), a _ {h} \sim \pi_ {\text {u n i f}}} \left[ \left(f \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right) - f ^ {\prime} \left(x _ {h}, a _ {h}\right)\right) ^ {2} \right] = \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2}.
$$

by taking a union bound over all  $f, f' \in \mathcal{f}_h$ , and by choosing  $\lambda = \varepsilon / h^2$ , we are guaranteed that

$$
(1 - \varepsilon) \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h} \leq \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \leq (1 + \varepsilon) \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h} \tag {106}
$$

for all  $f, f' \in \mathcal{f}_h$ . we now deduce the result for the star hull by homogeneity. consider  $f'' \in \mathrm{star}(\mathcal{f}_h, f)$ . we can write  $f'' = t(f' - f) + f$  for some  $f' \in \mathcal{f}_h$  and  $t \in [0,1]$ , so that  $f'' - f = t(f' - f)$ . (106) then implies that

$$
\| f - f ^ {\prime \prime} \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} = t ^ {2} \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \leq (1 + \varepsilon) t ^ {2} \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} + t ^ {2} c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h} \leq (1 + \varepsilon) \| f - f ^ {\prime \prime} \| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} + c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h},
$$

since  $t \leq 1$ . similarly, we have

$$
\| f - f ^ {\prime \prime} \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} = t ^ {2} \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {z} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} \geq (1 - \varepsilon) t ^ {2} \| f - f ^ {\prime} \| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} - t ^ {2} c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h} \geq (1 - \varepsilon) \| f - f ^ {\prime \prime} \| _ {\mathcal {l} _ {h} ^ {(k)}} ^ {2} - c _ {\varepsilon , \delta ; h},
$$

leading to the result.

proof of lemma g.3. let  $w \in \mathcal{w}_{h+1}^{(k)}$  be fixed, and recall that  $|w| \leq h^2$ . define  $z_j = w(x_{h+1}^{(j,h)})$  and

$$
\mathfrak {g} _ {j - 1} = \sigma (\mathcal {h} ^ {(1)}, \ldots , \mathcal {h} ^ {(j - 1)}, (s _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}, x _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}), \ldots , (s _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}), s _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}),
$$

with the convention  $\mathfrak{g}_0 = \sigma((s_1^{(j,h)}, x_1^{(j,h)}, a_1^{(j,h)}), \ldots, (s_h^{(j,h)}, x_h^{(j,h)}, a_h^{(j,h)}), s_{h+1}^{(j,h)})$ , where  $\mathcal{h}^{(j)}$  denotes the entire history for episode  $j$ . lemma b.2 implies that with probability at least  $1 - \delta$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j <   k} w \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}\right) = \sum_ {j <   k} z _ {j} \leq \frac {3}{2} \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} \left[ z _ {j} \mid \mathfrak {g} _ {j - 1} \right] + 4 h ^ {2} \log \left(2 \delta^ {- 1}\right) \\ = \frac {3}{2} \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} _ {x _ {h + 1} \sim \psi (s _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)})} [ w (x _ {h + 1}) ] + 4 h ^ {2} \log (2 \delta^ {- 1}). \\ \end{array}
$$

this proves the first statement for this choice of  $w$ . to prove the second statement, we define a new filtration

$$
\mathfrak {f} _ {j - 1} = \sigma (\mathcal {h} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \mathcal {h} ^ {(j - 1)}, (s _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}, x _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {1} ^ {(j, h)}), \dots , (s _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)})).
$$

lemma b.2 implies that with probability at least  $1 - \delta$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} \left[ w \left(x _ {h + 1}\right) \mid x _ {h} = x _ {h} ^ {(j, h)}, a _ {h} = a _ {h} ^ {(j, h)} \right] = \sum_ {j <   k} \mathbb {e} \left[ z _ {j} \mid \mathfrak {f} _ {j - 1} \right] \leq 2 \sum_ {j <   k} z _ {j} + 8 h ^ {2} \log (2 \delta^ {- 1}) \\ = 2 \sum_ {j <   k} w \left(x _ {h + 1} ^ {(j, h)}\right) + 8 h ^ {2} \log \left(2 \delta^ {- 1}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

the final result now follows from a union bound over all  $(2ek)^{s}|\mathcal{f}_{h + 1}|$  possible functions in  $\mathcal{w}_{h + 1}^{(k)}$ .
